A car pulled up right at the curb, not across the street, and a man in the driver's seat, older than thirty-seven, a little gray, waving with his whole arm out the window, embarrassingly early. A woman in the passenger seat with short hair and a pencil behind her ear. A little sister in the back, already talking before the door was open. *Val. Val. Why is the sky blue?* And in the courtyard, a transfer student with short hair and a face that didn't look like anyone but herself, standing by the wall, not sure where to go. And a girl with a pencil turning in her fingers, crossing to her, and saying, first, before she could—

"Hi."

It was there for the space of a breath.

Then it faded, gently, the way a picture fades in a window when the light outside comes up.

It had been a possible world. Val knew that. It wasn't hers, and it wasn't a lie either. It was one of the places things could go.

So was this one.

### Chapter 81B: Outside

She was standing on a train platform in the rain.

It wasn't Hakobune Central. She didn't know where it was. There was no seam in the sky, and no frost, and no cracked Moon bleeding red light across the clouds. There was only rain, gray and ordinary, coming down on a city she had never seen, at that hour before it's really morning, when the lights in the station are still on and the sky above the tracks is just starting to go pale.

People were waiting for the first train.

A man in a damp coat, eating something out of a paper bag. Two schoolgirls sharing one umbrella and arguing about whose fault it was that it was too small. An old woman with her shopping on the bench beside her. A station attendant in a blue cap, yawning. Nobody here had ever heard of a saint. Nobody here had ever seen a seal, or a Fall, or a giant of frost and glass over a harbor. Nobody had ever been asked to sit in a machine and be what it needed.

She looked at all of it for a long time.

It was the realest place she had been since the stair. She knew it the way Heaven had taught her to know things. Every bit of it had something wrong with it. A dent in the side of the vending machine where somebody had kicked it. A crack running through the platform tiles, patched with gray. The man's coat, wet through at the shoulders. One bent spoke in the schoolgirls' umbrella. The station clock running two minutes slow. Nothing here was perfect. Nothing here had been wished for. Everything here cast a shadow.

"Is that real?" said a small voice beside her.

"Every bit of it's got something wrong with it," Val said.

"Then it's real."

The old woman on the bench looked up as the station attendant went past, and said, "Morning," and he nodded, and yawned again. The schoolgirls went on arguing about the umbrella. The man with the paper bag folded it up small and put it in his pocket. None of them could see her. None of it was for her. They went on with their mornings, carelessly, the way you do when you don't know how much it's worth, and it was the most beautiful thing she had ever seen.

Far off down the line, a train was coming. She could hear it on the rails, getting closer.

### Chapter 82B: What Do You Want

*Valeria Kane.*

All three of them at once. The one she had named in a white room, who had asked six questions and then a seventh. The one Heaven had made out of frost because Val wanted her back so badly, who had asked four hundred questions at a kitchen table and kept asking when nobody answered. And the small new one, on the burned floor of the core, sitting on the lid of a fire so it would stay asleep. They had always been the same girl, the way a river is the same river.

Far above, on the frost of a whole world, the Root still stood with April's face, holding her want up to the door, the way it had held its own for a million years and never once gotten through.

*What do you want?*

Everyone had asked her that. Everyone on Earth, at the center, all at once, and she had answered them. She had wanted it to stop. It had stopped.

It wasn't what she wanted.

She thought of a dent in a vending machine. A bent spoke. An old woman saying *morning* to a man who only nodded. A rice ball pushed across a table. A meter of hangar step. A line drawn under her feet.

"I want to live," Val said.

She heard it come out. It didn't sound brave. It sounded small and true and a little stupid, the way the true things always did.

*What about everyone else?*

Val looked out past the edge of Heaven.

Past the white. Past the frost and the sea and the long bright stair and the chair at the top of it with nobody in it. Past the last thing anyone remembered, to the edge of all of it, where the light ran out, like the edge of a page.

There was someone there.

Not in Heaven. Not in the book. Outside it, holding it. She couldn't see a face. She didn't need to. Somebody had come all this way with her, past the plant and the port and the white room and the field, and was still here, at the end, with the light on, reading.

The question wasn't hers anymore. It went past her, out to the edge of the page.

*What about you?*

*What do you want?*

Val didn't answer for them. She couldn't. That was the whole point.

"They can choose," she said. To all of them in the frost. To everyone at the edge of everything. To you. "Anyone who wants to come back can come back. As themselves. I'm not going to want it for them. Not anymore."

She reached up and unclasped the Halo from her throat, and let it go, with its trigger, into the light, and didn't watch where it went.

### Chapter 83B: Picture Yourself

Yuki was still standing on the white, a meter away.

"How?" Val said. "How do they come back?"

Yuki considered it, very seriously.

"Anyone can come back," she said, "if they can picture themselves." She looked at Val's face as if she were reading it. "Not the way they see themselves. The way someone else does."

She waited.

"Can you?"

Val closed her eyes.

She tried to picture herself, and the girl at the desk came first, the way she always had: arms folded, head down, the class going on above her like weather. She let it come. She didn't push it away. She just didn't stop there.

A quiet girl by the window, who might answer someday.

A kid who needed feeding.

*Same thing.*

Four, crossed out. *Val.*

A girl in the passenger seat with a can of coffee going cold.

*Kiddo.*

A girl whose omelettes tore, so people would know they were hers.

The original.

Val-chan, holding on to an apron.

A girl at a doorway, so loved it frightened a grown man away.

*I'm your hero.*

They didn't add up. She held them all anyway, the way you hold an armful of things that don't match, and stood there on the line someone had drawn under her, and found that there was a shape in her arms after all. It wasn't the girl at the desk. It was bigger than that, and stranger, and it had a lot of things wrong with it, and it cast a shadow.

"I think so," Val said.

Yuki nodded, as if that were a fact like any other.

"What about you?" Val said. "Can you?"

Yuki looked down at her own hands.

"I've only ever had someone else's face," she said. "I want to know what mine is first."

She turned, and went, across the white, not quickly, toward a woman with short hair standing a long way off, who was waiting for her with a pencil in her hand and her own face, the original, which was not the same thing, and never had been.

Val watched her go. She didn't call after her. She didn't need to. She had seen what was in Yuki's eyes when she looked at Val, and it had been curiosity, and it had been kind, and it had been enough.

### Chapter 84B: Questions

*Do you want to live?*

"Yes."

*Why do you pilot?*

"So I could stand next to them."

*Who are you?*

"Val."

*What do you want?*

"To live. All of it. Even the wrong parts."

*Is anyone waiting for you?*

"Yes."

*Do you love anyone?*

"Yes."

*Does anyone love you?*

"...Yes."

*Should you have been born?*

"Yes."

*Will you say it back?*

"Yes," Val said.

Heaven cracked.

### Chapter 85B: Good Morning

It started under her feet, along the line.

A crack, thin and bright, running out from where she stood, the way ice cracks across a pond when someone steps on it. Then another. Then a hundred. They ran out across the white in every direction, branching, the way frost branches across a window, and she knew the sound they made. She had heard it a hundred times through 04, every time a seal broke.

This was the other sound. The one you never hear, because you're inside it. A seal coming back together. Hers. Closing around the shape she held in her arms, the picture of herself she had built out of everyone else's, and the white breaking open around it, because it couldn't hold her anymore.

And through the cracks, there were people.

They stood in a ring around her, on the other side of the breaking white, close. A meter away, every one of them. She couldn't have said whether they had been there all along, or whether she had only just learned how to see them. It didn't seem to matter.

Nanami was first. Of course she was. She had her clipboard under her arm and Hinata on her hip, and the smile she had smiled every morning in the doorway of Class 2-A, the one that had had so much practice at not being answered.

"Good morning, Kane-san!" Nanami said.

And then the rest of them, one after another, all the way around.

"Good morning," Akari said, with her arms folded. She didn't unfold them. She didn't look away, either.

"Morning," said Haruto. For once, he wasn't behind his camera.

"Good morning," said Kōta, out of breath, as if he had run there.

Tom said it with his mouth full, and pushed a rice ball across the air toward her, and grinned when she almost reached for it.

Sae said it out loud. Her voice was low, and a little hoarse, as if it hadn't been used in a very long time. Val had never heard it. She knew it anyway.

"Good morning, Val," said Kuze. There was no card in his hand.

"Good morning," said Dasha, and stopped there, and looked surprised at herself, and laughed.

Gage turned a bead in his fingers. "...Morning."

"Good morning, Kane-san," said Aoi Sena, warm and a little hoarse, with a pen stuck through her hair, exactly the way she had leaned on a kitchen counter once and said torn ones taste the same.

"Morning," said Alex. He had both arms. He was holding a plate of tamagoyaki, torn down the middle and burnt along one edge, and he held it out to her with both hands.

"Good morning, kiddo," said Maggie, and hummed the last bar of something old.

"Good morning," said a boy of fourteen with a face that was the same on both sides. Behind him, the children from the facility said it too, raggedly, not all at the same time.

"Good morning, Val," said Abriel, smiling, with frost on his shoulders.

"Good morning, Valeria," said Ruth Hermon, over her reading glasses.

"Good morning," said Eve Sakaki, stiffly, as if she had practiced it, and then, after a moment, she smiled.

"Good morning, Val-chan," said Fumi.

"Good morning, Valeria," said Yuki. Her face was a little different from the one Val remembered. It was a little more her own.

"...Morning," said Seus. He didn't look at her. Then he did.

"Good morning, Valeria Kane," said April, all three of her, at once.

"Valeria," said Patrick. He stood a little apart, in his coat, with his hands empty. "Good morning."

"Good morning," said her mother.

And her father, last. He said it the way a man says it at a school gate, a little embarrassed, as if he weren't sure he was allowed.

"Good morning, Valeria."

They were clapping.

All of them, around the ring. Steadily, the way people clap at the end of something long, and they didn't stop. Nanami with the clipboard under her arm. Tom with rice on his hands. Patrick, stiffly, the way he did anything for the first time. Her father, with his whole arms. The sound went on and on, around and around her, through the cracks in the white.

Val stood in the middle of all of them.

She was smiling.

She didn't know when it had started. It wasn't the crooked thing that had almost happened once on a hangar step. It came up from somewhere so far down that she hadn't known there was anything down there, and it took her whole face, and it didn't stop, and she didn't try to make it. Her face was wet. She was smiling so hard it hurt. She had never smiled like that in her life.

She looked at every one of them, all the way around the ring.

"Thank you," Val said.

*To my father, thank you.*

*To my mother, goodbye.*

*And to everyone who said it first: good morning.*

---

## Arc 20B: The Real World

> *While the earth remaineth, seedtime and harvest, and cold and heat, and summer and winter, and day and night shall not cease.*
>
> — Genesis 8:22

### Chapter 86B: The Ice

She woke up cold.

She was lying on her back on the ice at the lip of the shaft, in the ruins of headquarters, and the sky above her was dark.

It took her a long time to understand what was wrong with it. Then she did. There was no seam. For the first time since the Almost, there was no long bright wound from the hills to the top of the world. There was only sky, low and gray-black and ordinary, and behind it, very faint, the dim white coin of the sun. The Moon was still cracked. Nothing up there was looking down. Somewhere on the other side of it, someone was holding a door shut.

The light had come down while she was gone. It lay over everything now, as far as she could see, a fine glittering frost that didn't melt, faintly lit from inside, the way snow looks with a lamp buried under it. Over the ruins. Over the plain. Over the hills, all the way to where Fuji burned on the horizon, small and orange. Everybody on Earth was under it. Warm and soft and without edges, lying under the frost of the world like seeds under a field. Waiting, or not waiting.

The districts that had hung upside down over the city for seven years had come down while she was gone. Somebody had set them down, gently, still upside down, in the frost, the way you put toys away when you don't know which way up they go.

Across the shaft, the Vigil lay broken over the ruins where it had come down with nobody flying it, black now, not burning anymore, with the frost already growing over it like moss. She didn't look at it for long.

There were no exos anywhere. No white units. No Lizzie. Where the old orange unit had gone down, there was only a long scrape in the frost, and in the middle of it the shape of a capsule seat pressed into the ice, empty, and a line of small boot prints going away from it, south, toward the hills.

She touched her throat. It was bare. It felt colder than the rest of her.

Beside her, the frost moved.

It moved the way a blanket moves when someone under it turns over. It lifted, and cracked, and something came up out of it, gasping: a boy, sitting up all at once the way you sit up in bed when you've forgotten where you are, with the frost running off his hair and his shoulders and his face in glittering sheets, coughing, dragging in air as if he had been under water.

Seus.

He had been in there. She had seen him go, on a train, under his father's hand. And he had come back. Nobody had made him. She had said *they can choose*, and he was the first person on Earth who had.

He sat in the frost with his hands braced on the ice and breathed for a long time. He looked at the sky. He looked at the frost, and the upside-down school, and the dead ship across the shaft, and the empty scrape where Lizzie had been. His eye was just an eye. There was nothing behind it anymore.

Then he looked at her.

"You," he said.

She didn't say anything.

"I heard you." His voice was raw, scraped down to almost nothing. "In there. Everybody heard you. Everybody *was* you, for a while. I know what you said." He was shaking. "They asked you. All of us. Every person on Earth asked you what you wanted, and you said *stop.*"

"Yes," Val said.

"You ended the world because you were sad."

"Yes."

"My dad's in there." He said it as if it were someone else's sentence. "He held my head. He said *I'm here.* Twenty years, and the first time he ever says it, it's because you—" His voice broke and he didn't stop. "Alex is dead. He's lying in that ship. Aoi's dead. Tom's dead. Kuze's dead. Everybody who ever stood next to you, Kane, every single one, and you were in the middle of it, you had all of it in your hands, and you said *stop*, and then you said we could *choose.* Like it's a present. Like we're supposed to thank you."

She didn't look away from him. She didn't let herself.

"You never asked if I was alive," Seus said.

It came out of him quiet, and that was worse than the shouting.

"Not once. In the bay, on the plain, in the snow, at that stupid settlement, never. You never asked me if I was okay. You never asked me what I wanted. Nobody did. Nobody ever did. And then the one time anybody asks *you*—"

He came at her.

He had her down on the ice before she saw him move. His knee was on her chest and his hands were at her throat, where the Halo used to be, and this time he was squeezing.

She couldn't breathe.

She heard the ice creak under her head. She heard him, close, making a sound with no words in it, the sound Tom had made on the apron when Dasha fell. His hands were very cold and very strong and they didn't stop. The dark sky at the edges of her eyes went gray, and then grayer, and closed in, the way the white room had closed in once, fraying from the edges toward the middle.

She didn't fight him.

She could have. She was better than him; she had always been better than him. She didn't. She lay on the ice with his hands on her throat and the gray coming in, and she thought, very clearly, the way you think one thing at the bottom of deep water: *this is someone choosing too.*

Then she lifted her hand.

It was so heavy. It took everything she had left. She lifted it off the ice, slowly, the whole long way, and put it against his face.

She did it the way Maggie had once put a hand on her ankle, on the floor of a narrow steel room, and left it there. Not to stop anything. Not to fix anything. Only so there would be a hand. Her palm lay against his cheek, against the frost on it, against the wet, and she left it there.

His hands stopped.

They didn't let go. They stopped squeezing, and stayed where they were, and shook, and air went into her all at once, scraping, and she dragged it in and coughed and dragged it in again. Above her his face came apart. It went from the edges in, the way frost goes off a window, and he made a sound she had never heard him make, and his tears fell on her face, hot, one after another, and ran down into the frost under her head.

He didn't push her down anymore. He didn't let go of her either. He just stayed there, over her, on the ice, at the end of the world, with his hands at her throat and her hand on his face, and cried.

After a long time, he said, thickly:

"Your hand's freezing."

---

## Appendix A: v4 Changelog

What changed from the Revision 3 manuscript (`silence-in-heaven.md`, whose own changelog covers Revisions 1–3) and from Route B v2 (`silence-in-heaven-route-b-v2.md`):

- **Structure.** Arcs and chapters swap names: the 19 big units are Arcs (each with its epigraph), and the 80 small ones are Chapters. Same numbers. Ending B uses B numbers (Arcs 15B–20B, Chapters 66B onward).
- **New characters.** Yuki (the Rei equivalent), Ruth Hermon (Patrick's deputy, the Fuyutsuki equivalent) and Eve Sakaki (chief scientist, the Ritsuko equivalent).
- **New events.** Fall A (the swarm), Fall B (the glass), the three days soft in the port, Yuki's death in the raid, and Eve's tanks.
- **Two endings.** Ending A is the original ending with the v4 additions. Ending B is Route B v2 reordered, with a full Total Assimilation arc (19B) and the waking (20B).
- **The Aoi fix.** Val met Aoi in person, so nobody says she never saw her face.

Per-file details follow.

#### Arcs 1–3 (v4)
- **[structure]** Headings flipped in all three: `## Arc N` (with epigraph) over `### Chapter N`. Arc 1 is otherwise unchanged.
- **[Ruth]** Ch 4: Patrick's deputy, Ruth Hermon, sits half out of frame at the briefing ("a physics professor once"; says nothing; watches Patrick say things).
- **[Ruth]** Ch 6: after "Seal that footage," Patrick tells her "Ruth. Run the ops room. Don't ask."; she "had been not asking for thirteen years" (2076–2089) and takes his chair.
- **[Yuki]** Ch 4: introduced in the hangar on the lip of Unit 00's capsule (white, 00 on the hull), transferred in six months ago, never spoken to Val; looks at Val "the way you look at a word you almost recognize."
- **[Eve]** Interlude: the engineer who types *anomalous* is "a junior engineer named Eve Sakaki, Helen Enoch's student" (2082).
- **[Eve]** Ch 7: the Proxy page's last revision is signed *E. Sakaki, Chief of Research*.
- **[Yuki]** Ch 8: already in Class 2-A, bandaged wrist, no family name anyone knows; answers Nanami's "Good morning" once, flatly (her only good morning in the book). Val notices: "A girl who didn't seem to feel anything could say it, and she couldn't."
- **[Yuki]** Ch 8: Haruto's photos of her always come out blurred; "Are you two related?" / "No." / "No."
- **[Ruth]** Ch 12: does the drill's arithmetic on a duty roster and shows only Patrick.
- **[Eve]** Ch 12: "One and a half," on the long gun's shots; "never rounded up."
- **[Yuki]** Ch 12: Kōta sees Unit 00 on the shelter feed: "Is she scared?" / "I don't know. I don't think anyone's ever asked her."
- **[Yuki]** Ch 12: Unit 00 guards the long gun's power line in the valley; when the ice storm's edge reaches the cables, its seal goes up and holds. "Nobody saw it." The 04 shield beat is untouched.

#### Arc 4: Navis
- **Structure:** headings flipped. The big unit is now "Arc 4: Navis", and Chapters 13–16 plus the Stinger sit under it.
- **Fall A, "the swarm" (Ch 14):** a new scene the day the personnel notice goes up.
  - A Nephil breaks into thirty-eight bus-sized Nephilim over the business district.
  - Ruth Hermon counts them from Patrick's elbow.
  - Yuki holds Kōta's hospital alone in Unit 00 for eleven minutes, seal cracked, and takes one on her own arm ("Unit 00. Holding.").
  - Val arrives and finishes it. Kōta watches from a window.
  - Yuki reports "Pilot intact. ...Mostly." Eve Sakaki orders her pulled out.
- **Medical wing (Ch 14):** that night Val sees Patrick sitting by Yuki's bed, not speaking, looking at the wall above her face. Ruth arrives with two cups of tea: "Go to bed, Kane."
- **Corridor (Ch 14):** Patrick passes Seus coming "from the direction of the medical wing."
- **Gantry (Ch 14):** Seus climbs up to Yuki ("You held the hospital… That was good." / "Was it?" / "...Yeah. It was."), polite with her in a way he isn't with Val.
- **The mirror exchange (Ch 14):** Yuki: "What do you say when you don't know what to say?" Val: "I don't know. I never say anything." Yuki: "Then we're the same." Val sees "a face she felt she ought to know and couldn't place."
- **Eve (Ch 15):** the twin-Nephil analysis is now "Eve Sakaki's analysts."
- **Akari (Ch 16):** when a classmate calls Yuki "the doll," Akari says "She held the hospital… My brother watched her do it from the window," and starts eating lunch beside her.
- **Aoi (Ch 16):** Aoi packs Yuki's lunch every day ("Thank you, Lieutenant Sena." / "Aoi." / "Lieutenant Sena.").
- **Sunday dinners (Ch 16):** Yuki starts coming because Nanami tells her the time and address every Saturday.
- **Alex (Ch 16):** Alex teaches Yuki to fall in the sim room ("You're allowed to land.").
- **Stinger:** the Conclave adds "And the spare?" / "In place. Damaged in the swarm. Recovering."

#### Arc 5: The Dinner (v4)
- **Structure:** "Chapter 5" is now "Arc 5"; its sections are now Chapters 17–21.
- **Fall B, "the glass" (Ch 18):**
  - A Nephil lands on the school running track during third period, turning the ground to glass and walking toward the gym.
  - Val runs the two streets to headquarters, and 04 breaks the Nephil's seal in nine seconds without her deciding anything.
  - Yuki, still in her sling, watches from the shelter window.
  - In the ops room, Eve reads the sync numbers ("Ninety-eight. A hundred and four. A hundred and eleven.") and gives her warning: "Past a hundred, the seal can't tell where she ends. Pilots who go past it don't come out the same." Ruth is beside Patrick and says nothing.
  - Seus arrives forty seconds late. That night he pulls the sync record ("A hundred and eleven"), which is what sends him into the logs.
- **Yuki invited (Ch 19):** Seus suggests asking Yuki ("If there's four people at a table, he can't just get up and leave"). Val asks her at the shoe lockers. Yuki says "I'll come," echoing Patrick.
- **The port soft (Ch 20):**
  - While Proxy drives 04 into Navis, Val's sync climbs past a hundred and ten while Eve pleads to pull her out. Her seal opens, and she goes soft.
  - The recovery team finds an empty flight suit full of light. Eve: "Her seal isn't broken. It's open... 04 is holding her together. It's never done that for anyone."
  - Eve's salvage fails for three days, and she sits with Patrick's untouched coffee.
  - Yuki keeps a three-day vigil against 04's ankle. Aoi brings her lunch, Ruth brings tea, Alex comes and goes, and Patrick stops once at the hangar door and doesn't come in.
  - Inside the soft, everyone's voices ask the nine questions in the exact v4 order, and she answers all of them in the negative ("...No." to *Should you have been born?*, and no answer at all to *Will you say it back?*). The questions repeat until she notices that the voices asking are the people who decided everything for her.
  - Anger, not an answer, brings her back: "Who the fuck decides what's important for me?"
  - On the third morning, Yuki is first up the ladder and finds her curled on the capsule floor. The crying beat ("Not one tear... All of them") now happens there, with Yuki kneeling at the hatch. "Nobody had heard the scream. Her comms had been off for three days."
- **Show Something (Ch 21):**
  - It is now Sunday, three days after Thursday's dinner. Yuki walks Val home, sits in the chair set for the commander, and eats the cold torn tamagoyaki: "It tastes the same."
  - The table is set for four.
  - Val's voice is "wrecked... from three days of screaming nobody heard."
  - She decided "on the walk home" (was "on the train").
- **Unchanged:** Seus's last words, Aoi's second key, Alex seeing the vitals flicker, Lt. A. Sena's report, the goldfish, the pencil left in the desk, the gray car, and Maggie's arrival.

#### Arcs 6–8 (v4)

- **All three:** headings flipped (`## Arc N`, `### Chapter M`); numbers and epigraphs unchanged.

**Arc 6: Ask Me Later**
- Chapter 22, funeral: Ruth Hermon stands a step behind Patrick, looking at the photograph instead of at him. Yuki sits in the one folding chair anybody took, back straight, a fresh bandage on her wrist, because Dr. Sakaki told her that at a funeral you *sit*. She stays until the chairs are folded up around her. Patrick never looks at the front row.
- Chapter 22, debrief: after the junior officer's "bad luck", Eve Sakaki, without looking up from her tablet: "It wasn't luck. It was a corruption vector nobody modeled. My section didn't model it."
- Chapter 22, new scene: Nanami sends Val to Yuki's apartment with a laminated HOMEWORK — YUKI folder. The room is bare: a flat gray blanket, water and Dr. Sakaki's pill packets in the fridge, bandages on the sill, and Patrick's reading glasses with a cracked lens, kept like something in a shrine. ("He was sitting with me… He didn't ask for them back.") Val sees her own first apartment, says "You should eat something", hears whose line it is, and leaves.

**Arc 7: The Door**
- Chapter 26: Ruth stays at the ops desk and quietly closes the lower levels while Alex runs. Eve is at the observation glass of the drive wing, white-faced: "Get away from the glass. Those were never engines." Unit 00 (Yuki, in for a sync test) steps out of its bay and raises its seal over the stairwell the hangar crews fled up.
- Chapter 29: Unit 00 kneels at the foot of the stairs with its seal cracked edge to edge. Yuki asks Val "Are you injured?" in 04's exact voice, and answers Val's "No" with "Okay," trying the word out. Ruth took the hold-fire call herself and won't say who it was. Eve signs the order to cover the drives and doesn't look at anyone.

**Arc 8: The Third War on Heaven, Almost**
- Chapter 31, new beat: Conclave troops come down the hangar stairs after the barefoot Val. Unit 00 steps between them and raises its seal across the hangar, and the frost pouring off where 04 stood climbs its legs. Yuki: "Go." Val runs without looking back. On the channel, using the name for the first time: **"Valeria. I think this is what I want."** Then a seal breaking, and silence.
- Chapter 31, new beat: on the command board the 00 square goes gray. Only Ruth, standing behind Patrick's chair, sees his hand close on the armrest until the knuckles whiten.
- Chapter 32: passing the hangar door in Navis, Val doesn't look through it.
- Chapter 35 (double guilt seeded): in the white room, among the reasons Val can't say yes, she thinks of the white unit kneeling with its seal broken and nobody inside, a voice saying *I think this is what I want*, and how she didn't look back: "She had left her there to come here."
- The sword and door mechanics in Chapters 34–35 are untouched.

#### Arcs 9–12

- **Structure (all four):** headings flipped. `## Arc N` for the big units (9 After, 10 Ararat, 11 Lanterns, 12 Ararat Falls) and `### Chapter N` for the small ones (36–49). Numbers unchanged; epigraphs kept under the arcs.
- **Arc 9, Ch 38:** Seus's accusation gains Yuki: "Yuki held the hangar for you so you could do it. Did anybody tell you that? Four in the morning, in that old white unit, until there was nothing left of it."
- **Arc 9, Ch 39:** in the frozen Class 2-A, Yuki's desk is two rows over with frost across its lid; Val doesn't touch it and opens her own.
- **Arc 10, Ch 40:** after the failed "And—" endings, a new paragraph: she never tries an ending on the other one (the white unit at the hangar doors at four in the morning, the flat voice saying her whole name, Val going past it toward a girl she then lost anyway). "There was no sentence for that."
- **Arc 10, Ch 43:** after Gage's "Everybody who stands next to you dies," Val's lesson lists "Dasha, on the apron. Yuki, at the hangar doors, seven years ago, holding them for her."
- **Arc 11, Ch 46:** at the rice dinner, after Gage and Kuze say the names, Val adds one under her breath that nobody at the table knew: *Yuki.*
- **Arc 12, Ch 49:** Val recognizes the white unit as Unit 00, Yuki's: the narrow shoulders, the old first-generation joints, the numbers painted off its shoulders so only two pale ovals show if you know to look. For one second something in her lifts its head before the capsule opens on a girl with April's face. Yuki is added to the end of Val's list of the dead ("And before any of them, Yuki, at the hangar doors at four in the morning"). The interior designation 00 now reads: "Somebody had painted it off the outside. They had left it in here."
- Ruth and Eve stay in Patrick's Umbral; no WAKE-scene appearances added.

#### Arcs 13–15

- **Structure:** headings flipped (Arc 13–15 for the big units, Chapters 50–66 for the small ones).
- **Ch 50:** the flight in Unit 00 notes the capsule "had been built for Yuki."

A go stone clicks in the corner. The next paragraph now reads "he didn't care about this one at all."

  - The face is Sora Yukimura's, grown from cells taken from her body after the test. Val remembers she had sat beside it for months, and Haruto's "Are you related?" / "No."

- **Ch 64:** Val makes six lanterns, the sixth reading YUKI (no family name). Before April's ABRIEL lantern, Val floats Yuki's: "She held a door at four in the morning so I could go and get you back. And I didn't get you back... She had my mother's face. I didn't know until it was too late to tell her." / "Did she know?" / "I don't think anybody ever told her anything." The cluster is now seven lanterns.

Val says it back to every one. Each steps aside, and the last opening shows the burning house and her mother. No clapping.

## Appendix B: Story Bible

A quick reference for keeping the next draft consistent. In v4 the big units are **Arcs** and the small ones are **Chapters**. Anything marked *(new)* was invented during revision and can be changed freely.

### The rules in one place

- **The seal.** Every living thing with a self has one: the boundary that keeps you *you*, and keeps you *in*. Saints, Nephilim, exo cores and humans all have one.
  - Exos raise their seals as shields: a pane of pale light with frost lines running through it, which rings when struck and cracks like ice on a pond when it breaks.
  - Guns can't get through a Nephil's seal. Only another seal can break it, which is why only exos can fight Nephilim.
  - Nobody outside the Conclave knows humans have seals until Ch 16 (Palindrome hints at it in Ch 5).
- **Going soft.** Pilots' slang for brain death: a pilot's seal breaking inside the capsule. The mind runs out like warm wax.
- **Total Assimilation (the Rite).** Every seal on Earth stripped at once, and the whole world going soft together into one sea of light. It isn't death. It doesn't think or feel; it just is. "Nobody in it was anybody."
- **Heaven.**
  - A higher plane made of thought and memory, outside time, with no physics. It can be shaped by will and gives you what you want most.
  - It can't tell what happened from what you wish had happened. The tell: real memories have something wrong in them; wished ones are flawless and cast no shadows; the in-between ones flicker.
  - It touches the universe only through one door, which can't be opened or closed without a war on Heaven. The door is never a physical thing. It opens in the air right above whatever is opening it.
  - The throne at the top of the stair is empty. Whether anyone ever sat on it is never answered.
- **The swords.**
  - Older than anyone knows. Half alive, and they answer what people want.
  - One is driven into every saint and keeps it asleep. Pulling one out starts a war on Heaven nobody can control (2044). The Conclave files those as *swords of despair*; their cold fire flickers like a flame in wind.
  - Sariel's sword is filed as *the sword of hope*. It was never driven into Sariel; Sariel was holding it, which is why taking it woke his child and not him. It burns perfectly steady, and starts to flicker when a want turns it (the Third War, the Fourth).
  - The rule: a sword keeps a door shut, unless someone near it wants it open more. Hope does the opposite job and shuts a door that's open.
- **The opener.** Human flesh, nephil flesh and Semjaza flesh merged in one body, with a sword as the key. A sword that gives way goes into the opener like a key into a lock and turns, and the door opens above it. A second sword turned the other way inside it locks the door again.
- **Who stays awake in Heaven.** The Rite took everyone who didn't choose. Val refused it; Sam and Sora were there before it; Patrick walked in; Maggie flew in. Everyone else is seen in their perfect state, the thing they wanted most, just before they blur into one.
- **Aging.** "Their flesh keeps you, once it's in you past the skin." Maggie (her accident), Val (04, deeper than the link) and Seus (the piece of Penemue in his eye). Graft relics don't stop aging.

### Timeline

| 2042 | Patrick Moriah born. |

The sky goes white for three days (the cover story is a "solar superflare") and the colonies are evacuated. A piece breaks off and falls to Earth; when the sword is driven back in, it goes into that piece. The piece becomes the Root, sealed in the Reliquary under Hakobune. |
| 2044 onward | The wars after the white sky: nations fighting over what was left, and colony refugees nobody wanted. |

| ~2047 | Maggie born. |

He massacres the Graft children; Palindrome (14) reverses his shot and Tom (10) is missed in a basement tank. The Conclave, suspecting him, has been poisoning him. Sam dies at 37. Maggie's standing order. |

The plant, the new school, Navis, the dinner, the Proxy, the door, the raid, then the Almost (Trumpet Seven): the Root's sword gives way to Val and goes into Navis, the door opens above her, and Abriel's Hope turns the key back and stays in the lock, and the seam freezes half open. Navis dies; Aoi dies; Val is sealed in the Beast. |

It grows Unit 12 from the Root over the same years, and by the end leaves the station to Proxy guards. Seus wakes after a year and a half and flies a new core. WAKE builds the Vigil, and Ararat grows. |
| 2096 | Part Two. The Morningstar falls on Fuji. Ararat and Lantern, then Ararat's fall. The Third War: Hope turns, then steadies, and Abriel dies closing the door. The Settlement in Kasumi. The Fourth War: Patrick opens the door, the Rite, Heaven, and the Rewrite. |
| After | The rewritten world. The Moon is whole and the saints are resting. Only Seus remembers. |

### Unit roster

| Unit | Pilot | Notes |

| 00 | Yuki (2089), then Zero-Four (2096) | White first-generation test unit. Patrick had the numbers painted off after Yuki died; Zero-Four flies it unnumbered. |
| 01 Lizzie | Maggie | Orange, first generation. |
| 02 | Hisashi Kuze | Flew off Sam's wing in the montage years. |
| 03 | Hayashi *(new)* | Sam's other wingman, now dead. |
| 04 | the intruder (Penemue), then Sam (PIPSTICK), then Val | The only exo grown from Root (Semjaza) flesh. See the layer map. |
| 05 | Sae Kirishima | Its nature went soft instead of her in the Almost, mid-song. |
| 06 | Abriel | "The silver one." Carried Hope. |
| 07 Ross | Alex | Third generation. Alex's arm was crushed by the capsule wall. |
| 08 Navis | Seus | Died in the Almost. Its body plus 04's core became the Beast. |
| "Navis" (new) | Seus | A blank core Seus assimilated after waking, in a salvaged chassis. He saved it at the Morningstar and named it Navis. Not the same being; it talks like Navis because Seus hasn't changed. |
| 12 | Abriel and Val, then Patrick | Grown from the Root over seven years: the second Semjaza body. |
| hollows | WAKE pilots | White units with the mind cut out. They keep "little habits in the meat." |

**How exos work.**
- The chassis is the body (a shirt anyone can wear). The core is the exo. The nature is who the core becomes.
- A newborn core is a blank slate, and assimilation makes it a mirror of its pilot. That mirror is a feedback loop: a sad pilot makes a sad exo, which makes the pilot sadder.
- An assimilated core has a mind of its own. It can resist another pilot and can only be replaced, never re-assimilated.
- The link joins one exo and one pilot. Damage to the exo comes through as pain and numbness, and it's gone when the link breaks.

### Lantern Team (WAKE)

Roll call order: "One. Two. Three. Five. Six. Four." Four is called last because Val was added to the card last. Before Val, the last Four was Ikeda *(new)*.

| Callsign | Name | Death |
|---|---|---|
| One | Capt. Hisashi Kuze | Rams a bomber at Ararat: "Val. Your name's Val. I remembered." |

| Five | Sae Kirishima | Takes Val's seat on transport A (Ch 11). Her last transmission is three clicks: *I'm here*. |
| Six | Gage Hart | "I still hate you. Go." (Ch 12). |

### What's inside 04 (and the three Aprils)

1. **The core.** Root (Semjaza) flesh.
2. **The intruder's trace (Penemue).** 04's first pilot. His daughter Mio named the exo Pipstick.
3. **Pip.** The nature Sam shaped, a being of its own. He learned Sam's plan, then let April grow knowing he'd have to make room. He links Val in Ch 1 and deletes himself: "There wasn't room for both of us."
4. **The rage.** Sam's plan, a weapon he built. It is not Sam.
5. **April.** A nature Sam made in his last year, shaped to what he thought Val would need. It is not Sam's consciousness.

**The three Aprils** are all 04:
- **April 1:** the nature Val named in the white room.
- **April 2 (Zero-Four):** a frost body Heaven built in the Almost because Val wanted April back. She has April's face (with a bit of Sam's in it) and none of April's memories. Her mind is human, so the more human she becomes, the less frost she makes, and she melts away in warm Kasumi.
- **April 3:** 04's core starting over from nothing ("Who are you?" / "I'm your hero").

They merge in Heaven. In the new world, Val's little sister is named April.

### The loops

- The world has played these years over countless times, and only Abriel remembers. He can't remember who first asked him to make Valeria happy.
- Each loop ended with Val in Heaven asking for it all to be taken back, and Heaven, which gives you what you want most, gave her a restart. Each time, Abriel offered her a fix.
- This time he told her to answer April. Dying in her arms, he understood the difference, and his changed want closed the door.
- In Heaven the lunar dawn breaks his loop first.

### Glossary

- **Sync:** live connection strength, a percentage.
- **Proxy:** mission control drives a unit through what's left of its nature, using two keys. It can move a body at a distance; driving the rage means reaching into the core in person. Umbral built it.
- **Halo:** WAKE's kill collar. Alex holds the trigger; range 60 km.
- **Trumpets:** alert levels. Three is a Fall over water, Four is the darkening, and Seven was declared in the Almost.
- **Fall:** a Nephil coming down.
- **Nephilim:** the saints' children, made by the saints' want to get into Heaven. They think Heaven is home.
- **Saints:** Sariel (inside the Moon), Azazel (shattered into the belt; every exo but 04 and 12 is grown from it), and Semjaza (Europa; the leader, and the only one that can open the door). The Testament calls them devils.
- **Jinn:** street slang for people grafted with saint relics.
- **Corruption:** frost-veined infection of exo tissue.
- **Umbral:** a military branch under the country's government. After the Almost it is Patrick's, and it still owns the Morningstar.
- **WAKE:** Alex's force after the Almost.
- **The Conclave:** a shadow body above all of them.
  - They want salvation through Total Assimilation: every war on Heaven ends a world, people kill each other in between, and the seals are where loneliness and war begin.
  - They can't control people or events; they arrange roads.
  - Their Testament lists events, not how they happen. It ends at the Rite: "There is no after."
- **The warp drives:** built around the Root, a crack leaning on the one door. They open one way only.
- **The wars on Heaven:**
  - First: the saints, long ago.
  - Second: 2044.
  - The Almost: 2089.
  - Third: 2096, Unit 12 and Hope.
  - Fourth: 2096, Patrick.

### Places

- **Hakobune:** the ark city, built over the Root. It has headquarters, the Reliquary (the huge basement under headquarters where the Root is), Hakobune Municipal No. 3 (Class 2-A), the harbor and Hakobune Central station.
- **The Morningstar:** Umbral's orbital gun station. It comes down on Mount Fuji in 2096, and Fuji burns a long time after.
- **Ararat:** WAKE's caldera town around the Vigil.
- **The Vigil:** WAKE's flying ship.
- **Kasumi:** the warm hot-spring valley where the Settlement grows. Nanami walked 112 people out of the city to it.
- **Fumi's house:** a narrow house on the east side of Hakobune with a persimmon tree. Letters went "c/o Fumi Takada" *(new surname)*.

### Themes to keep straight

- **Silence and how you answer it.** The saints answered Heaven's silence with war. Sam answered a six-year-old's silence by staying away. April 2 kept asking anyway; the icebox tally reads four hundred and something under QUESTIONS and nothing under ANSWERS.
- **Val's line in Heaven's book:** "No silence is the last word." She writes it with her mother's pencil and leaves the pencil, and the past, in the book; Sora, who stays behind, keeps it. The Testament had every page scheduled; her book has one line.
- **Choice versus obligation.**
  - Patrick held "Hold."
  - Abriel held a request from a face he can't remember.
  - Maggie came for Val at the end because "Nobody asked me this time."
  - Seus chose to remember.
- **Left open on purpose:** whether the people in the new world are the same people, and whether anyone ever sat on the throne.

### New in v4

**People**
- **Yuki.** Umbral's other child pilot in 2089, Unit 00. The Conclave's spare ("Vessel line, Subject 02"), grown by Eve Sakaki from Root flesh and cells taken from Sora's body after Test 2075-11, so she has **Sora's face**. Quiet, literal: "I was made in case." Dies in the Arc 8 raid holding the hangar so Val can reach Navis: "Valeria. I think this is what I want." Val's guilt is double: Yuki died for a rescue that failed. Her origin is revealed in Arc 13, at Eve's tanks.
- **Ruth Hermon.** Deputy commander of Umbral, sixties, once Sora's thesis adviser at the Space Transit Research Division. The chain of holding: Sam told Patrick "Hold"; Patrick tells Ruth "Run the ops room. Don't ask." She goes soft alone at the ops desk during the Rite: "Yukimura."
- **Eve Sakaki.** Umbral's chief scientist, Helen Enoch's student and successor (the cradle, natures, Proxy, the vessel line). Loves Patrick, who looks at Sora's face on Yuki instead. Destroys the remaining spares in Arc 13: "They're nobody. I made them nobody." Tries to lock hangar zero's cradle in the Fourth War. Lives in Ending A; killed by the Conclave's troops in Ending B.

**Events**
- **Fall A, "the swarm" (Arc 4).** A Nephil breaks into dozens of bus-sized ones over the city; Yuki holds Kōta's hospital alone in Unit 00 and is hurt.
- **Fall B, "the glass" (Arc 5).** A Nephil in the school yard turns the ground to glass; Val's sync goes past a hundred. Eve: "Past a hundred, the seal can't tell where she ends."
- **The port (Arc 5).** Under Patrick's Proxy, Val goes soft inside the capsule for three days, a small Total Assimilation. 04 holds her together ("It's never done that for anyone"). She answers every question with no, and comes back on the third day out of anger: "Who the fuck decides what's important for me?"

**The port questions, and Ending B's answers**

| Question | In the port | In Ending B |
|---|---|---|
| Do you want to live? | No. | Yes. |
| Why do you pilot? | It's the normal thing to do. | So I could stand next to them. |
| Who are you? | Nobody. | Val. |
| What do you want? | Nothing. | To live. All of it. Even the wrong parts. |
| Is anyone waiting for you? | No. | Yes. |
| Do you love anyone? | No. | Yes. |
| Does anyone love you? | No. | ...Yes. |
| Should you have been born? | ...No. | Yes. |
| Will you say it back? | (no answer) | Yes. |

**The two endings**
- **Ending A, the Rewrite:** Arcs 16–19 and the Epilogue. A good-morning ring after the if-only rooms opens onto Sora, with Yuki beside her ("You don't have to wear my face anymore"). Val writes one line in Heaven's book. The Root gave her its dream, which is why Heaven listens. The new world has a transfer student with a face of her own.
- **Ending B, the Real World:** branches at the end of Arc 15. Val never recovers from April's death and goes back to take everything back. The Conclave's troops board the Vigil, Alex dies on the hangar floor, and Navis is torn apart. At the center, the Root rises out of the Reliquary with April's face. Semjaza, which wanted the door for a million years, hands Val its dream and carries her "I want it to stop" to the door. In Arc 19B (the Total Assimilation through Val's eyes), she learns she believes she shouldn't have existed, then sees the world without her and herself in everyone's eyes, and finds it's okay to live. Yuki tells her anyone can come back who can picture themselves the way someone else does. The good-morning ring comes through the cracks of Heaven, everyone clapping, and she wakes on the ice with Seus.
